finite constants
                                 Km < ∞,       0 ≤ m ≤ 3,

on one reproduced weak ball.

                        Km ≤ Bm K≤m    , K≤m  , K≤m   , K≤m  , K≤m   , K≤m    ,
where Bm is the finite multivariable Bell polynomial supplied by the chain rule through order m.
Proof. Compose the six bounded maps. Through order three, the chain rule contains only finitely
many partitions. Every term is bounded in the same permanent κ-weighted norm and carries the same
active/source inventory.
   The input allocation cone is an assumption of the weak activity ball; the output cone is derived after
the exact connected output by recertification. Choose the ball so
                                                  C(uω + r) ≤ b∗
and the derived output allocation is at most b∗ . This is an invariant ball inequality, not circular use of
the output theorem as an input.

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.
   In the canonical squared-coupling normalization,

                                                       11 CA (G) log 2
                                               bAF =                   .
                                                           24π 2
The factor log 2 is the physical scale-two interval. The complete coordinate-intertwining theorem is
printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

5.2     Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

5.2.3 Measure-level intertwining
For each scheduled step let Bj∗ be the exact Karcher pushforward from scale aj to aj+1 , including
canonical rescaling and exact regular/irregular label recombination. Define the corresponding finite-
volume standard-scheme map by
                                     Σj gaj (µ) := gaj+1 (Bj∗ µ).                               (17)

This evaluates the local renormalization condition after the exact physical pushforward and does not
push a gauge condition through the block. Let Σ∞ = Σstd denote the autonomous rooted-thermodynamic
comparison obtained from the same local functional. The scheduled comparison through the quadratic
jet is O(δG (Mj )u2 + u3 ) + O(u−p e−c/u ) through the two background marks, and becomes OG (u3j ) after
evaluation on an admissible directed orbit.
Theorem 5.6 (Nonautonomous full-measure coordinate intertwining). For the fixed tail-compatible
schedule s and all sufficiently small u,

                             hj+1 ◦ Fj = Σj ◦ hj         for every scheduled step j.                       (18)

directed orbit it is OG (u3j ) in value and OG (u2j ) after one scalar derivative. No vanishing finite-volume

is Lemma 5.3 and U4. Gauge fixing occurs only inside the local definition of g after equality of the

quotient kernel or supportwise annihilation theorem is substituted for it.

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of
HX has zero complete local jet below engineering degree five. In the reflection-even physical background
two-point sector, degree five is absent by signed-hypercubic reflection, so the first nonzero associated-graded

insertion, the one-loop identity L = I − V + 1 = 1 shows that every additional quantum vertex is
a tree attachment to the unique loop; after those tree attachments are collected, the insertion enters
the background two-point amplitude through one effective two-quantum-leg kernel. Two fluctuation
derivatives choose an ordered pair of edges in X, producing at most C|X|2 terms. Rooted parallel
transport and finite localization cost only a support polynomial. Reflection excludes an odd engineering
degree in the physical scalar two-point sector.
Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Definition 5.11 (Lowenstein infrared dimension at the coefficient interface). Give a vector, background-
vector, ghost, or antighost leg infrared dimension one, and give a lattice difference and the Lowenstein
variable 1 − s infrared dimension one. For a periodic vertex symbol V let νIR (V ) be its lower Taylor order
when all incident soft momenta and 1 − s are scaled together, and put

                               ρ(V ) := νIR (V ) + nA (V ) + nc (V ) + nc̄ (V ),

where background-vector legs are counted in nA . The SI–7M vertex condition used here is

                                                   ρ(V ) ≥ 4                                           (20)

for every elementary or reduced local vertex entering a Lowenstein forest. For a connected soft graph γ
whose massless vector and ghost lines have order −2, incidence and Lγ = Iγ − |Vγ | + 1 give
                                          X                        X

Lemma 5.12 (Complete infrared vertex and forest admission). Fix X with a diam X ≤ 1, a graph in

                                                X

  Every elementary vertex and every reduced vertex admitted by the forest satisfies (20). More explicitly,

                       vertex family                 νIR       number of dimension-one legs    ρ
             vector or ghost quadratic symbol         2                     2                  4
           three-vector or ghost–vector vertex       ≥1                     3                 ≥4
         four-vector, seagull, or tadpole vertex     ≥0                     4                 ≥4
             pure-Wilson reduced local vertex       4−n                     n                  4
           loop-bearing marked reduced vertex       ≥6−n                    n                 ≥ 6.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the
complete list of soft degrees is

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                   ω ghost bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,
                                  ω seagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

   The pure-Wilson ultraviolet degrees are the SI–6 degrees. By the one-cycle ledger, a mixed contraction

the finite SI–7M sector decomposition, uniformly in the periodic side M and through the two marks, the

Proof. The quadratic, cubic, quartic, gauge-fixing, and ghost rows are the finite Taylor rows of the local

                    (2)

Proof. Fix one support and q ∈ Q⋆ , keeping background, quantum, and ghost momenta separate. Apply
the complete SI–6B/SI–7M packet and the graph classification of Lemma 5.10. Lemma 5.12 verifies, for
every complete packet and through the two consumed background marks, lattice periodicity, denominator

Lemma 5.15 (Scale-independent finite scheme map). Let gW be the local standard coupling on the

Proof. Evaluate the same canonically rescaled nonexceptional projector P⋆ on the two full weak families.

after the step. The displayed conjugacy follows from the definitions.

5.3    Domain compactness and chart invariance
Let Ba,N be the rooted activity space with norm

Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,
                                 X      ′                        ′

uniformly in F ∈ F.
  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is
uniform, and the compact embedding
                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

6.1     Directed volume schedule and scalar transversality
For a trajectory of n weak dyadic steps, choose a terminal coarse torus of side Mout and put

                                         uj+1 ≤ uj + Bu2j

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.
   In the canonical squared-coupling normalization,

                                                       11 CA (G) log 2
                                               bAF =                   .
                                                           24π 2
The factor log 2 is the physical scale-two interval. The complete coordinate-intertwining theorem is
printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

5.2     Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

5.2.3 Measure-level intertwining
For each scheduled step let Bj∗ be the exact Karcher pushforward from scale aj to aj+1 , including
canonical rescaling and exact regular/irregular label recombination. Define the corresponding finite-
volume standard-scheme map by
                                     Σj gaj (µ) := gaj+1 (Bj∗ µ).                               (17)
                                               

This evaluates the local renormalization condition after the exact physical pushforward and does not
push a gauge condition through the block. Let Σ∞ = Σstd denote the autonomous rooted-thermodynamic
comparison obtained from the same local functional. The scheduled comparison through the quadratic
jet is O(δG (Mj )u2 + u3 ) + O(u−p e−c/u ) through the two background marks, and becomes OG (u3j ) after
evaluation on an admissible directed orbit.
Theorem 5.6 (Nonautonomous full-measure coordinate intertwining). For the fixed tail-compatible
schedule s and all sufficiently small u,

                             hj+1 ◦ Fj = Σj ◦ hj         for every scheduled step j.                       (18)

The autonomous identity is only the common C 3 endpoint comparison. For another schedule or the
regular-only inner-chart measure, the quadratic-jet comparison is the row of Lemma 5.5. On an admissible
directed orbit it is OG (u3j ) in value and OG (u2j ) after one scalar derivative. No vanishing finite-volume
two-jet and no exponential acoustic wrapping row is asserted.
Proof. The definition of the exact scheduled RG step gives the literal finite-volume measure identity

                                                bsj,u = µ
                                            Bj∗ µ       bsj+1,Fj (u) .

The coarea density, normal-fiber Jacobian, gauge-coordinate density, determinant activity, regular sector,
periodic wrapping term, and exact irregular-label sum occur once each. Applying the scale-aj+1 local
functional gives (18). The comparison with a second schedule is Lemma 5.5; the regular-only comparison
is Lemma 5.3 and U4. Gauge fixing occurs only inside the local definition of g after equality of the
physical measures, so no global gauge-orbit pushforward identity is used.

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

5.3    Domain compactness and chart invariance
Let Ba,N be the rooted activity space with norm

                                                  e

where each KX is a compact finite-dimensional product chart and, for every m, only finitely many rooted

Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,
                                 X      ′                        ′
                            sup       ea |X| ∥FX ∥C N −1 ≤ e−(a−a )L M,
                                  e

                                       |X|>L

uniformly in F ∈ F.
  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is
uniform, and the compact embedding
                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on
all shapes of size at most L. Letting L increase and using the uniform tail estimate proves relative
compactness in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

The finite-volume normal chart, strict macrostep contraction, and irregular Fréchet flatness used below
are not separate inputs: they are respectively Theorem 2.5, Theorem 4.30, and Theorem 3.31.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.



                                                        49
Proposition 5.24 (Finite-order marked RG bridge to the associated-graded block). Let

                                           TD (u, ϱ) : J≤D −→ J≤D

give a coupling derivative of the complementary contribution bounded by u−p e−c/u , which extends by
zero continuously at the endpoint. Hence the complete finite matrix is C 1 at (0, 0).
   At the endpoint the exact block is the Gaussian/Wick block. Wick localization preserves the highest-

                                      ∥TD (u, ϱ) − LD ∥ ≤ CD (u + ∥ϱ∥),

and the centered cone gives the final O(u) row. No analytic extension in the singular coupling variable is

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff
D, there are normalized source coordinates in the weak regime such that:
   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running
       logarithm;
  (ii) the complementary scalar source block is strictly contracting;
 (iii) a dimension-∆ + 2 defect relative to a principal dimension-∆ source contributes

                                                     r2 L(r)pD

      at physical scale r;
 (iv) all fixed marked rows obey uniform finite Schwartz-seminorm bounds.
  This theorem stops at the compact first-crossing handoff. It does not invoke the later macrobridge or
matching transfer.
Proof. Normalize each principal block by the fundamental matrix in Lemma 5.25. The strict complemen-
tary contraction sums the triangular inhomogeneous forcing by a geometric series. Canonical dimensional
scaling supplies the factor r2 for a degree-∆ + 2 defect; conjugation by the principal frame contributes at
most L(r)pD . The finite marked fiber and the exact marked one-step bounds give the stated Schwartz
estimates.

5.2.3 Measure-level intertwining
For each scheduled step let Bj∗ be the exact Karcher pushforward from scale aj to aj+1 , including

                                     Σj gaj (µ) := gaj+1 (Bj∗ µ).                               (17)

This evaluates the local renormalization condition after the exact physical pushforward and does not

comparison obtained from the same local functional. The scheduled comparison through the quadratic
jet is O(δG (Mj )u2 + u3 ) + O(u−p e−c/u ) through the two background marks, and becomes OG (u3j ) after

                             hj+1 ◦ Fj = Σj ◦ hj         for every scheduled step j.                       (18)

directed orbit it is OG (u3j ) in value and OG (u2j ) after one scalar derivative. No vanishing finite-volume

is Lemma 5.3 and U4. Gauge fixing occurs only inside the local definition of g after equality of the

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of
HX has zero complete local jet below engineering degree five. In the reflection-even physical background

transport and finite localization cost only a support polynomial. Reflection excludes an odd engineering
degree in the physical scalar two-point sector.
Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.

operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,
                                 X      ′                        ′

uniformly in F ∈ F.
  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is
uniform, and the compact embedding
                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on
all shapes of size at most L. Letting L increase and using the uniform tail estimate proves relative
compactness in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff
D, there are normalized source coordinates in the weak regime such that:
   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running
       logarithm;

                                                     r2 L(r)pD

      at physical scale r;

  This theorem stops at the compact first-crossing handoff. It does not invoke the later macrobridge or
matching transfer.
Proof. Normalize each principal block by the fundamental matrix in Lemma 5.25. The strict complemen-
tary contraction sums the triangular inhomogeneous forcing by a geometric series. Canonical dimensional
scaling supplies the factor r2 for a degree-∆ + 2 defect; conjugation by the principal frame contributes at
most L(r)pD . The finite marked fiber and the exact marked one-step bounds give the stated Schwartz
estimates.

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.

The exponentially flat irregular term is absorbed in the same cubic row after shrinking the weak interval.
The graph transform on weighted C 2 graphs has Lipschitz constant q + C v̄ < 1. Its unique fixed point
gives the graph, and the mean-value theorem gives centered contraction.

5.2     Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of

Proof. The exponential row is the differentiated invariant-graph bound with an unused support reserve.

the marked B 2 A2 row that invalidates an unmarked centering argument. At first order in the tangent
insertion, the one-loop identity L = I − V + 1 = 1 shows that every additional quantum vertex is

transport and finite localization cost only a support polynomial. Reflection excludes an odd engineering
degree in the physical scalar two-point sector.
Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest
theorem, not estimated as a separated Taylor piece. In particular, for the one-loop two-point classes the
complete list of soft degrees is

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Proof. The quadratic, cubic, quartic, gauge-fixing, and ghost rows are the finite Taylor rows of the local

Lemma 5.13 (Finite-support marked power-counting consequence). Let HX satisfy (19). For a diam X ≤
1, the BPHZL-subtracted one-loop nonexceptional background packet has the form
                    (2)
                   IX (a, q⋆ ) = AX (q⋆ ) + O a2 | log a| C(1 + diam X)q1 ∥HX ∥a;3,2 ,         (24)
                                                         

                                       (2)
where AX (q⋆ ) is finite and [log a] P⋆ IX = 0. Supports with a diam X > 1 have rooted total O(aN ) for
every fixed N .
Proof. Fix one support and q ∈ Q⋆ , keeping background, quantum, and ghost momenta separate. Apply
the complete SI–6B/SI–7M packet and the graph classification of Lemma 5.10. Lemma 5.12 verifies, for
every complete packet and through the two consumed background marks, lattice periodicity, denominator
scaling, numerator smoothness, all overall and proper-subgraph degrees, the pure-Wilson/loop-free-
local/loop-bearing marked trichotomy, support-polynomial control, and uniform rooted domination.
SI–7M is therefore applicable to every complete loop-bearing post-contraction packet. Pure-Wilson
proper divergent subgraphs are removed in the SI–6 scheme, while every quotient that still contains
the distinguished stable insertion has irrelevant excess at least two. Lemma 5.9 gives a finite sum of
two-quantum-leg symbols whose complete marked local Taylor jet starts at engineering degree d ≥ 6. The
complete marked Taylor polynomial of the insertion through degree four is zero by (19). Subtractions
may produce local power rows but cannot restore a marginal input exponent; the remaining symbol
carries ad−4 .
   On each fixed unit loop-momentum annulus, the verified SI–7/SI–7M hypotheses give the uniform
marked bound used below. Write the ultraviolet annuli as |k| ≍ 2j with 0 ≤ j ≤ J(a) = O(| log a|). A
marked component of engineering degree d contributes to P⋆ with an explicit ad−4 and an annulus row
bounded by C2(d−4)j . If d > 4, the annulus sum is geometric:
                                                   J(a)
                                                   X
                                         a   d−4
                                                          2(d−4)j = O(1),
                                                   j=0

so it produces a finite local scheme row and no logarithm. A logarithm occurs only at the marginal
exponent d − 4 = 0. Higher external-momentum Taylor coefficients can lower the annulus exponent to
zero, but then they retain the explicit ad−4 ; for d ≥ 6 their logarithmic row is O(a2 | log a|). Hence an
unsuppressed P⋆ log a term would require a nonzero marked degree-four input jet, which is absent. No
assertion that a redundant dimension-four insertion is annihilated support by support is used: such rows
have already been extracted and are not present in HX .
   Finite-support multiplicity is polynomial in diam X; for a diam X > 1, the strict support reserve
dominates every prescribed power of a.

one-loop nonexceptional background packet is an absolutely and uniformly convergent rooted sum of (24).
For some finite q,
                           (2)

Thus the full centered invariant family differs from the canonically normalized Wilson family by one finite,

Proof. The support polynomial in Lemma 5.13 is absorbed by the strict exponent reserve. The finite-
support amplitudes and their two background derivatives may therefore be summed before removing the
cutoff. At the quadratic order in the marginal coordinate needed for the one-step coefficient, apply the
complete SI–6B/SI–7M lattice BPHZL packet, the exhaustive ledger of Lemma 5.10, and the complete
post-contraction admission check of Lemma 5.12 to every mixed Wilson/stable term. A marginal proper
subgraph containing only Wilson vertices is exactly a standard background-field subgraph and is subtracted
in the SI–6 scheme. After all such subgraphs are contracted, every remaining quotient graph contains at
least one stable marked insertion and hence has positive irrelevant excess, at least two in the reflection-even
physical sector. Its overall annulus sum is therefore a power row or an explicitly a2 | log a|-suppressed
row, not an unsuppressed logarithm. Multiple stable insertions only increase that excess. The marked
stable-fiber identity removes every degree-four input row before this forest analysis. Uniform convergence
leaves one finite C 2 local scheme term and no new unsuppressed logarithmic coefficient.
Lemma 5.15 (Scale-independent finite scheme map). Let gW be the local standard coupling on the
canonically rescaled pure Wilson family and let g⋆ be the same local functional on the full centered
invariant family. On the common weak interval there is one C 2 map
                                  s(g) = g + cs g 2 + O(g 3 ),      s′ (0) = 1,

The C 3 -flat irregular complement is included in s; no analyticity at g = 0 is claimed or needed.
Proof. Evaluate the same canonically rescaled nonexceptional projector P⋆ on the two full weak families.
Their tree Hessians coincide. Their first difference is the invariant-graph tangent uH1 , whose vacuum
scalar and complete dimension-four retained projection vanish. Lemma 5.14 proves, rather than assumes,
that the entire exponentially quasi-local insertion family contributes a finite scale-independent transverse
scheme row and no new unsuppressed log a coefficient. SI–6 excludes an independent background
dimension-four counterterm. The inner-chart difference is therefore a finite C 2 unit-tangent redefinition.
U4 adds a C 3 -flat term through the two consumed background marks, which changes neither s′ (0) nor its
quadratic coefficient. Canonical rescaling identifies the profile, gauge fermion, subtraction projector, and
cutoff with the same unit-lattice template at every dyadic scale, so the same map s occurs before and
after the step. The displayed conjugacy follows from the definitions.

Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,

  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is

                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff

   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running

                                                     r2 L(r)pD

matching transfer.

After the two background marks are taken, every finite-support background/quantum/ghost kernel of
HX has zero complete local jet below engineering degree five. In the reflection-even physical background

Proof. The exponential row is the differentiated invariant-graph bound with an unused support reserve.

the marked B 2 A2 row that invalidates an unmarked centering argument. At first order in the tangent

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

The two background marks create external legs and do not differentiate external momentum, so they do
not lower these orders. A mixed forest contraction is loop-free. A pure-Wilson contraction produces only
a standard dimension-four background-field vertex, while every quotient that retains the stable insertion
retains its degree-six marked vertex.
   Consequently every subtraction-generated exceptional configuration is covered by the SI–7M forest

                                   ω vector bubble = 4 − 2 · 2 + 1 + 1 = 2,

These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein

packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Lemma 5.15 (Scale-independent finite scheme map). Let gW be the local standard coupling on the

5.3    Domain compactness and chart invariance
Let Ba,N be the rooted activity space with norm
                                               X

                                                  e

where each KX is a compact finite-dimensional product chart and, for every m, only finitely many rooted
connected support shapes of size at most m occur.
Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,
                                 X      ′                        ′
                            sup       ea |X| ∥FX ∥C N −1 ≤ e−(a−a )L M,
                                  e
                                        X∋e
                                       |X|>L

uniformly in F ∈ F.
  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is
uniform, and the compact embedding
                                           C N (KX ) ⋐ C N −1 (KX )
follows from Arzela–Ascoli in finitely many coordinate charts. A diagonal subsequence converges on
all shapes of size at most L. Letting L increase and using the uniform tail estimate proves relative
compactness in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.



                                                        49

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff
D, there are normalized source coordinates in the weak regime such that:
   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running
       logarithm;
  (ii) the complementary scalar source block is strictly contracting;
 (iii) a dimension-∆ + 2 defect relative to a principal dimension-∆ source contributes

                                                     r2 L(r)pD

      at physical scale r;
 (iv) all fixed marked rows obey uniform finite Schwartz-seminorm bounds.
  This theorem stops at the compact first-crossing handoff. It does not invoke the later macrobridge or
matching transfer.
Proof. Normalize each principal block by the fundamental matrix in Lemma 5.25. The strict complemen-
tary contraction sums the triangular inhomogeneous forcing by a geometric series. Canonical dimensional
scaling supplies the factor r2 for a degree-∆ + 2 defect; conjugation by the principal frame contributes at
most L(r)pD . The finite marked fiber and the exact marked one-step bounds give the stated Schwartz
estimates.

5.6     Ultraviolet closure theorem
Fix an arbitrary passive-source order N < ∞. Activities carry at most three active structural derivatives,
source derivatives through order N , and zero, one, or two passive exterior roots.
Theorem 5.27 (Uniform centered ultraviolet renormalization). There are a rooted support exponent

6.4    Finite-volume Perron input
Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,
the one-step matching transfer kernel relative to product Haar is continuous, symmetric, and strictly
positive. If MΛ is the finite number of one-link updates in a sweep, then
                                                          −1
                                          KL (x, y) ≥ ϵM
                                                       ⋆ ZΛ,L > 0.
                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .

with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A


                                                             44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN ) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Theorem 5.18 (Compact weakening). If a > 0, a′ > 0, and a′ < a, every bounded subset of Ba,N ,
N ≥ 1, is relatively compact in Ba′ ,N −1 .
Proof. Let F be bounded by M in Ba,N . For a cutoff L,

  For |X| ≤ L, only finitely many rooted support shapes occur. On each shape, the C N (KX ) bound is

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1 (w, z1 ) and Ψ2 (w, z2 ) be two exact C 1 fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U ) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F ,
                      Z                                   Z
                         F (Ψ1 (w, z1 )) J1 (w, z1 ) dz1 = F (Ψ2 (w, z2 )) J2 (w, z2 ) dz2 .

Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.


                                                        48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

are not separate inputs: they are respectively Theorem 2.5, Theorem 4.30, and Theorem 3.31.

5.5    Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
                                                      M
                                               J≤D =       J∆ .
                                                         ∆≤D

The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF , hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.



                                                        49

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff
D, there are normalized source coordinates in the weak regime such that:
   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running
       logarithm;
  (ii) the complementary scalar source block is strictly contracting;
 (iii) a dimension-∆ + 2 defect relative to a principal dimension-∆ source contributes

                                                     r2 L(r)pD

      at physical scale r;
 (iv) all fixed marked rows obey uniform finite Schwartz-seminorm bounds.
  This theorem stops at the compact first-crossing handoff. It does not invoke the later macrobridge or
matching transfer.
Proof. Normalize each principal block by the fundamental matrix in Lemma 5.25. The strict complemen-
tary contraction sums the triangular inhomogeneous forcing by a geometric series. Canonical dimensional
scaling supplies the factor r2 for a degree-∆ + 2 defect; conjugation by the principal frame contributes at
most L(r)pD . The finite marked fiber and the exact marked one-step bounds give the stated Schwartz
estimates.

connector. The factor inventory is exhaustive: Wilson words, product-Haar coordinate factors, coarea

each history by one canonical hull. The history-compatibility theorem computes the next allocation only
from this collected output, so neither a factor nor its scalar normalization is copied at a later depth. This
gives (HT1) at j + 1. The normalized structural/source marks commute with the finite induction and are
bounded by the same permanent rows, proving depth uniformity.

6.4    Finite-volume Perron input
Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,
the one-step matching transfer kernel relative to product Haar is continuous, symmetric, and strictly
positive. If MΛ is the finite number of one-link updates in a sweep, then
                                                          −1

                                                       ⋆ ZΛ,L > 0.
                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.
Lemma 6.14 (Unit-Haar macroblock disintegration). For every path,
                                L
                                Y                              L
                                                               Y
                                      dmG (Uek ) = dmG (Wγ )         dmG (Zγ,k ).
                                k=1                            k=2

Consequently the simultaneous path-product map has unit Haar Jacobian, is gauge covariant at path
endpoints, and intertwines aligned reflection.
Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.
Definition 6.15 (Carrier-adapted hybrid chart). Let A be the finite set of retained macro-links carrying

the underlying exact pushforward.

A     Endpoint packet-shell verification
This appendix supplies the detailed proof of Theorem 10.4. It is part of the same manuscript and
uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint
theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance,
and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem.

Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ,
the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is
reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division
                                                      X
                                    Tλ (p) − Tλ (0) =    pbα pbβ Sλ,αβ (p).                        (188)
                                                       α,β

For every 0 < a′ < a0 , the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same
complete packet at exponent a0 .
   If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the
canonical relative factor
                                             (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)
with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned
to a chosen branch or attachment cut.
Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color,
translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg
coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated
after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0.
Reflection gives Tλ (−p) = Tλ (p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor
formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum
derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ .
   The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension
four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this
observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
   The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.
